Improved estimation via model selection method for semimartingale regressions based on discrete data Journal: Journal of Multivariate Analysis
Evgeny Pchelintsev, Serguei Pergamenshchikov, Maria Povzun

Abstract
In this paper we study a high dimension (Big Data) semimartingale regression model observed in the discrete time moments. We study this model in a nonparametric setting. To this end improved (shrinkage) estimation methods are developed and the non-asymptotic comparison between shrinkage and least squares estimates are studied. The nonparametric improvement effect for the shrinkage estimates showing the significant advantage with respect to the parametric case is established. Then, an model selection method based on these estimates is developed. Non-asymptotic sharp oracle inequalities for the constructed model selection procedure are obtained. Constructive sufficient conditions for the observation frequency providing the robust efficiency property in adaptive setting are found. As an example, the regression model with non-Gaussian Ornstein–Uhlenbeck–Lévy noises is considered. The results of Monte-Carlo simulations are given which confirm numerically the obtained theoretical results.



2010 MSC
62G08 , 62G05

1
Introduction

1.1
Problem and motivations

In this paper we consider the linear regression model in continuous time

\mathrm{d}y_{\mathchoice{t}{t}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t$}}}=\,\sum^{q}_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}\beta_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}{\bf u}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t+\mathrm{d}\xi_{\mathchoice{t}{t}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t$}}}\,,\quad 0\leq t\leq n\,,

(1.1)

where
({\bf u}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{1\leq j\leq q}{1\leq j\leq q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq j\leq q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq j\leq q$}}}
is a system of known linear independent

1
-periodic
{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}
functions, the noise process
(\xi_{\mathchoice{t}{t}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t$}}})_{\mathchoice{t\geq 0}{t\geq 0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t\geq 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t\geq 0$}}}
is an
unobservable semimartingale with unknown distribution.
The process
(
1.1
) is observed only at the discrete time moments

(y_{\mathchoice{t_{j}}{t_{j}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t_{j}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t_{j}$}}})_{\mathchoice{0\leq j\leq N}{0\leq j\leq N}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0\leq j\leq N$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0\leq j\leq N$}}}\,,\quad t_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}=\frac{j}{p}\quad\mbox{and}\quad N=np\,,

(1.2)

where the observations frequency
p
is some fixed integer number. We consider the model (
1.1
) in the big data setting, i.e. under condition that
parameter dimension is more than number of observations, i.e.
q>N
.
Usually, in these cases
for statistical models with independent observations one uses the following methods: Lasso algorithm (see, for example,

[
37
]
)
or the Dantzig selector method proposed in
[
6
]
.
For dependent observations such models
were studied, for example, in
[
9
]
and
[
11
]
.
But in all these papers the number of parameters
q
is known and, therefore, unfortunately,
these methods can’t be used to estimate, for example, the
number of parameters
q
.
It should be noted, that the case when the number of parameters
q
is unknown is one of challenging problems
in the signal and image processing theory
(see, for example,
[
1
,
4
]
and the references therein).
In this paper, similar to

[
14
,
15
]
,
we study this problem in nonparametric setting which
allows us to consider the models (
1.1
) with
unknown
q
or with
q=+\infty
.
So, we consider the following observations model

\mathrm{d}y_{t}=S(t)\mathrm{d}t+\mathrm{d}\xi_{\mathchoice{t}{t}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t$}}}\,,\quad 0\leq t\leq n\,,

(1.3)

where
S
is an unknown
1
-periodic
{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}

function
from
{\bf L}_{\mathchoice{2}{2}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}}[0,1]
.

Note that if
(\xi_{\mathchoice{t}{t}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t$}}})_{\mathchoice{t\geq 0}{t\geq 0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t\geq 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t\geq 0$}}}
is a Brownian motion, then we obtain the "signal plus white noise" model (see, for example,
[
17
,
28
,
29
]
and etc.)
which is widely used in statistical radiophysics. In the case, when the the process
(\xi_{\mathchoice{t}{t}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t$}}})_{\mathchoice{t\geq 0}{t\geq 0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t\geq 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t\geq 0$}}}

is a semimartingale, the models (
1.3
) are
very popular in important practice problems such that, for example,
signals and images processing
[
2
,
4
,
20
]
,
finance and insurance (see, for example,

[
3
,
5
,
19
]
and the references therein).
For the first time the nonparametric regression models (
1.3
) with general semimartingale noises
were introduced in
[
22
,
23
]
. Here the
adaptive efficient robust estimation methods based on the model selection approach are developed.
More precisely, to take into account the dependent observations in the framework of the models
(
1.3
) in the papers

[
16
,
21
,
24
]

it was used the Ornstein–Uhlenbeck noise processes, so called color Gaussian noises.
To consider non-Gaussian observations
it was introduced
in the papers

[
2
,
25
,
26
,
27
,
31
]

impulse noises defined through the semi-Markov or Lévy processes.
Moreover, for similar models in the papers

[
32
,
33
,
34
,
36
,
39
]

improved (shrinkage) nonparametric estimation procedures have been developed that significantly improve the
non asymptotic estimation quality compared with usual methods.
It should be emphasized, that in all these papers the improved estimation problems are studied only for the complete observations cases, i.e. when the all trajectory
(y_{\mathchoice{t}{t}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t$}}})_{\mathchoice{0\leq t\leq n}{0\leq t\leq n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0\leq t\leq n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0\leq t\leq n$}}}
is available for the observations. Therefore, these results can’t be apply to study the model (
1.1
)
in big data setting.

1.2
Main contributions

Our main goal in this paper is to develop improved estimation methods
for the model (
1.1
) on the basis of the observations (
1.2
).
As an example for
(\xi_{\mathchoice{t}{t}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t$}}})_{\mathchoice{t\geq 0}{t\geq 0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t\geq 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t\geq 0$}}}
, we consider the noise defined by non-Gaussian Ornstein–Uhlenbeck process with unknown distribution.
In the general framework of the observations model (
1.3
) we develop special model selection methods based on the improved weighted least squares estimates.
We recall, that for the first time such approach was proposed in
[
10
]
for spherical symmetric regression models in discrete time and in

[
24
]
for Gaussian regression models in continuous time. It should be noted that for
the non spherically symmetric distributions we can not use directly the well-known improved (shrinkage) estimators proposed in
[
18
]
. To apply the improved estimation methods to the general regression models in continuous time one needs to use the modifications of the James – Stein shrinkage procedure proposed in
[
27
,
31
]
for parametric models and developed in

[
32
,
33
,
34
,
36
,
39
]
for nonparametric ones.
Next, in this paper we study the estimation accuracy improvement effect based on discrete data (
1.2
).
We show that the proposed improved estimators outperform the ordinary least squares estimates
in non-asymptotic accuracy estimation uniformly over the observation frequency
p\geq 1
.
It turns out, that in this case the estimation improvement effect is the same as for the complete observations case, i.e. it is established that uniformly over the observation frequency
the improvement of the non-asymptotic accuracy is much more significant than for parametric models, since according to the well-known James - Stein formula, the accuracy improvement increases when the parametric dimension increases (see,
[
33
]
). Recall, that for the parametric models this dimension is always fixed, while for the nonparametric ones it tends to infinity, that is, it becomes arbitrarily large with an increase in the number of observations. Therefore, the gain from the application of improved methods is essentially increasing with respect to the parametric case. In the next step, we develop a special adaptive estimation tool
through the model selection approach. More precisely, we find constructive sufficient conditions for the observation frequency and for the noise distributions under which
through the robust risks
we show sharp non-asymptotic oracle inequalities for the proposed improved model selection procedures.
Finally, using these oracle inequalities, we provide the efficiency property for these procedures in adaptive setting.

1.3
Plan of the paper

The rest of the paper is organized as follows.
In Section
2
we give all necessary conditions which are used for the model (
1.3
). In Section
3
we construct the shrinkage
weighted least squares estimates and study the improvement effect. In Section
4
we
develop improved model selection method. In Section
5
we announce the main results of this paper.
A numerical Monte-Carlo analysis is given in Section
6
. The main properties of the non-Gaussian Ornstein–Uhlenbeck processes are studied in
Section
7
. Section
8
contains the proofs of the all main results.
In the appendix (Section
A
) it is postponed all auxiliary technical results.

2
Main conditions

First we assume that, the noise process
(\xi_{\mathchoice{t}{t}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t$}}})_{\mathchoice{t\geq 0}{t\geq 0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t\geq 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t\geq 0$}}}
in the model (
1.3
)
is a square integrable semimartingale with the values in the
Skorokhod space
{\bf D}[0,n]

such that, for any
function
f
from
{\bf L}_{\mathchoice{2}{2}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}}[0,n]
, the stochastic integral

I_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(f)=\int^{n}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}f(s)\mathrm{d}\xi_{\mathchoice{s}{s}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle s$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle s$}}}

(2.1)

has the following properties

{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q}{Q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q$}}}I_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(f)=0\quad\mbox{and}\quad{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q}{Q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q$}}}I^{2}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(f)\leq\varkappa_{\mathchoice{Q}{Q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q$}}}\int^{n}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}\,f^{2}(s)\mathrm{d}s\,.

(2.2)

Here
{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q}{Q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q$}}}
denotes the expectation with respect to the distribution

Q
of the noise process
(\xi_{\mathchoice{t}{t}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t$}}})_{\mathchoice{0\leq t\leq n}{0\leq t\leq n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0\leq t\leq n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0\leq t\leq n$}}}
on the space
{\bf D}[0,n]
,

\varkappa_{\mathchoice{Q}{Q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q$}}}>0
is
some positive constant depending on the distribution
Q
. As to the noise distribution
Q
we assume that it is unknown and belongs to some
family
{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}
of probability distributions in
{\bf D}[0,n]
.
For this problem we use the quadratic risk, which for any estimate
\widehat{S}
, is defined as

{\cal R}_{\mathchoice{Q}{Q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q$}}}(\widehat{S},S):={\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q,S}{Q,S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,S$}}}\,\|\widehat{S}-S\|^{2}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\|f\|^{2}:=\int^{1}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}f^{2}(t)\mathrm{d}t\,,

(2.3)

where
{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q,S}{Q,S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,S$}}}
stands for the expectation with respect to the
distribution
{\bf P}_{\mathchoice{Q,S}{Q,S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,S$}}}
of the process (
1.3
) with a fixed
distribution
Q
of the noise
(\xi_{\mathchoice{t}{t}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t$}}})_{\mathchoice{0\leq t\leq n}{0\leq t\leq n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0\leq t\leq n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0\leq t\leq n$}}}

and a given function
S
. Moreover, in the case when the distribution

Q
is unknown
we use also the robust risk

{\cal R}^{*}(\widehat{S},S)=\sup_{\mathchoice{Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}}\,{\cal R}_{\mathchoice{Q}{Q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q$}}}(\widehat{S},S)\,.

(2.4)

For the distribution family
{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}
we assume the following condition

{\bf H}_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}})

The family
{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}

is such that

\varkappa^{*}=\varkappa^{*}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}=\sup_{\mathchoice{Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}}\,\varkappa_{\mathchoice{Q}{Q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q$}}}<\infty

and for any
\epsilon>0

\lim_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}\,n^{-\epsilon}\,\varkappa^{*}=0\,.

Now we consider the noise
(\xi_{\mathchoice{t}{t}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t$}}})_{\mathchoice{t\geq 0}{t\geq 0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t\geq 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t\geq 0$}}}
in
(
1.3
) defined by a non-Gaussian Ornstein–Uhlenbeck process. Such processes are used in
the financial Black–Scholes type markets with jumps (see, for
example,
[
3
]
, and the references therein). Let the noise
process in (
1.3
) obeys the equation

\mathrm{d}\xi_{\mathchoice{t}{t}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t$}}}=a\xi_{\mathchoice{t}{t}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t$}}}\mathrm{d}t+\mathrm{d}u_{\mathchoice{t}{t}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t$}}}\,,\quad\xi_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}=0\,,

(2.5)

where

u_{\mathchoice{t}{t}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t$}}}=\varrho_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}}\,w_{\mathchoice{t}{t}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t$}}}+\varrho_{\mathchoice{2}{2}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}}\,z_{\mathchoice{t}{t}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t$}}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad z_{\mathchoice{t}{t}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t$}}}=x*(\mu-\widetilde{\mu})_{\mathchoice{t}{t}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t$}}}\,.

Here
(w_{\mathchoice{t}{t}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t$}}})_{\mathchoice{t\geq 0}{t\geq 0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t\geq 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t\geq 0$}}}
is
a standard Brownian motion,
"
*
" denotes the stochastic integral with respect to the compensated jump measure
\mu(\mathrm{d}s\,,\,\mathrm{d}x)
with deterministic
compensator
\widetilde{\mu}(\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}x)=\mathrm{d}s\Pi(\mathrm{d}x)
, i.e.

z_{\mathchoice{t}{t}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t$}}}=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}v\,(\mu-\widetilde{\mu})(\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}v)\quad\mbox{and}\quad{\mathbb{R}}_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}={\mathbb{R}}\setminus\{0\}\,,

\Pi(\cdot)
is the Lévy measure on
{\mathbb{R}}_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}
, (see, for example in
[
7
]
), such that

\Pi(x^{2})=1\quad\mbox{and}\quad\Pi(x^{8})\,<\,\infty\,.

(2.6)

We use the notation
\Pi(|x|^{m})=\int_{\mathchoice{{\mathbb{R}}_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}{{\mathbb{R}}_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathbb{R}}_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathbb{R}}_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}$}}}\,|z|^{m}\,\Pi(\mathrm{d}z)
.
Moreover, we assume that the nuisance parameters
a\leq 0
,
\varrho_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}}

and
\varrho_{\mathchoice{2}{2}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}}
satisfy the conditions

-a_{\mathchoice{max}{max}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle max$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle max$}}}\leq a\leq 0\,,\quad 0<\underline{\varrho}\leq\varrho^{2}_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\sigma_{\mathchoice{Q}{Q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q$}}}=\varrho^{2}_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}}+\varrho^{2}_{\mathchoice{2}{2}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}}\,\leq\varsigma^{*}\,,

(2.7)

where
the bounds

a_{\mathchoice{max}{max}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle max$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle max$}}}
,
\underline{\varrho}
and
\varsigma^{*}
are functions of
n
, i.e.

a_{\mathchoice{max}{max}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle max$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle max$}}}=a_{\mathchoice{max}{max}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle max$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle max$}}}(n)
,

\underline{\varrho}=\varrho_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}

and
\varsigma^{*}=\varsigma^{*}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}
, such that for any
\epsilon>0

\lim_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}\,\frac{a_{\mathchoice{max}{max}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle max$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle max$}}}(n)+\varsigma^{*}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{n^{\epsilon}}=0\quad\mbox{and}\quad\liminf_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}\,n^{\epsilon}\,\underline{\varrho}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}>0\,.

(2.8)

In this case
{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}
is the family
of all distributions of process (
1.3
) – (
2.5
) on the Skorokhod space

{\bf D}[0,n]
satisfying the conditions (
2.7
) – (
2.8
).
It should be noted that in view of Corollary
A.2
and the last inequality in (
2.7
),
the condition (
2.2
) for the process (
2.5
) holds with

\varkappa_{\mathchoice{Q}{Q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q$}}}=2\varsigma^{*}
.
Note also that the process
(
2.5
) is conditionally-Gaussian square integrable semimartingale with respect to

\sigma
-algebra
{\cal G}=\sigma\{z_{\mathchoice{t}{t}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t$}}}\,,\,t\geq 0\}
which is generated by jump process
(z_{t})_{t\geq 0}
.

3
Improved estimation method

For estimating the unknown function
S
in (
1.3
) we will use it’s Fourier expansion
on the time grid
\{t_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}},\ldots,t_{\mathchoice{p}{p}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}}}\}
defined in (
1.2
). To this end we chose basic
1
- periodic
functions
(\phi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{1\leq j\leq p}{1\leq j\leq p}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq j\leq p$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq j\leq p$}}}
from
{\bf L}_{\mathchoice{2}{2}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}}[0,1]
, which are orthonormal on this grid, i.e.

(\phi_{\mathchoice{i}{i}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle i$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i$}}},\phi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{p}=\frac{1}{p}\sum_{\mathchoice{l=1}{l=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l=1$}}}^{p}\,\phi_{\mathchoice{i}{i}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle i$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i$}}}(t_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}})\phi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(t_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}})={\bf 1}_{\{i=j\}}\,.

(3.1)

Note that we do not require the functions
(\phi_{\mathchoice{1\leq j\leq p}{1\leq j\leq p}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq j\leq p$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq j\leq p$}}})
to be orthogonal in
{\bf L}_{\mathchoice{2}{2}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}}[0,1]
, it suffices to have only the property (
3.1
).
Assume that these functions are bounded, i.e.
for some constant
\phi_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}\geq 1
, which may be depend on
n
,

\sup_{\mathchoice{0\leq j\leq n}{0\leq j\leq n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0\leq j\leq n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0\leq j\leq n$}}}\,\sup_{\mathchoice{0\leq t\leq 1}{0\leq t\leq 1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0\leq t\leq 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0\leq t\leq 1$}}}|\phi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(t)|\,\leq\,\phi_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}<\infty\,.

(3.2)

We can take, for example, the Demmler - Reinsh spline basis defined in
[
8
]
.
In the sequel we will use the trigonometric functions
(\mbox{Tr}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{j\geq 1}{j\geq 1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j\geq 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j\geq 1$}}}
.
We recall that
\mbox{Tr}_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}}\equiv 1
and for
j\geq 2

\mbox{Tr}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(x)=\sqrt{2}\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\cos(2\pi[j/2]x)\,\quad\mbox{for even}\quad j\,;\\[11.38109pt]
\sin(2\pi[j/2]x)\quad\mbox{for odd}\quad j\,.\end{array}\right.

(3.3)

Here
[a]
denotes the integer part of
a
. It should be noted
that the trigonometric basis possesses the property (
3.1
)
only in the case when
p
is odd. It is clear that if
p
is even we can always to reduce the observation to
p-1
.
Note now that for any
t\in\{t_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}},\ldots,t_{\mathchoice{p}{p}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}}}\}

we can represent the unknown function as

S(t)=\sum_{j=1}^{p}\theta_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\phi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(t)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\theta_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}=(S,\phi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{p}{p}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}}}\,.

(3.4)

Therefore, to estimate the function
S
it suffices to estimate the coefficients
\theta_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}
.
To this end we note that

\theta_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}=(S,\phi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{p}{p}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}}}=\sum^{p}_{\mathchoice{l=1}{l=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l=1$}}}\,\phi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(t_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}})S(t_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}})(t_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}-t_{\mathchoice{l-1}{l-1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l-1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l-1$}}})\,,

and it is nature to replace the difference

S(t_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}})(t_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}-t_{\mathchoice{l-1}{l-1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l-1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l-1$}}})
by the observation from the model (
1.3
)
as
y_{\mathchoice{t_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}}{t_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}$}}}-y_{\mathchoice{t_{\mathchoice{l-1}{l-1}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l-1$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l-1$}}}}{t_{\mathchoice{l-1}{l-1}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l-1$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l-1$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t_{\mathchoice{l-1}{l-1}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l-1$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l-1$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t_{\mathchoice{l-1}{l-1}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l-1$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l-1$}}}$}}}
. It should be note here also that to obtain a "good"
estimator one needs to use all observations
(y_{\mathchoice{t_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}}{t_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}$}}})_{\mathchoice{1\leq l\leq np}{1\leq l\leq np}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq l\leq np$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq l\leq np$}}}
. Since
the functions
\phi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}
and
S
are
1
periodic, i.e. for any integers
1\leq l\leq p
and
1\leq k\leq n

\phi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(t_{\mathchoice{l+kp}{l+kp}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l+kp$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l+kp$}}})=\phi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(t_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}})\quad\mbox{and}\quad S(t_{\mathchoice{l+kp}{l+kp}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l+kp$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l+kp$}}})=S(t_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}})\,,

therefore, we can represent the coefficients in (
3.4
) as

\theta_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}=(S,\phi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{p}{p}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}}}=\frac{1}{n}\sum^{np}_{\mathchoice{l=1}{l=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l=1$}}}\,\phi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(t_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}})S(t_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}})(t_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}-t_{\mathchoice{l-1}{l-1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l-1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l-1$}}})\,.

Using here the observations
(
1.2
), we define the estimator as

\widehat{\theta}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}=\frac{1}{n}\sum^{np}_{\mathchoice{l=1}{l=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l=1$}}}\,\phi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(t_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}})\,(y_{\mathchoice{t_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}}{t_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}$}}}-y_{\mathchoice{t_{\mathchoice{l-1}{l-1}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l-1$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l-1$}}}}{t_{\mathchoice{l-1}{l-1}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l-1$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l-1$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t_{\mathchoice{l-1}{l-1}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l-1$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l-1$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t_{\mathchoice{l-1}{l-1}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l-1$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l-1$}}}$}}})=\frac{1}{n}\int^{n}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}\,\psi_{j}(t)\,\mathrm{d}y_{\mathchoice{t}{t}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t$}}}\,

(3.5)

with

\psi_{j}(t)=\sum_{k=1}^{np}\phi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(t_{k}){\bf 1}_{\mathchoice{(t_{k-1},t_{k}]}{(t_{k-1},t_{k}]}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle(t_{k-1},t_{k}]$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle(t_{k-1},t_{k}]$}}}(t)\,.

For any functions
f,g
from
{\bf L}_{\mathchoice{2}{2}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}}[0,1]
we denote by
(f,g)
the inner product in
{\bf L}_{\mathchoice{2}{2}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}}[0,1]
.
The system of the functions
(\psi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{1\leq j\leq p}{1\leq j\leq p}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq j\leq p$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq j\leq p$}}}

is orthonormal in
{\bf L}_{\mathchoice{2}{2}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}}[0,1]
, i.e.

(\psi_{\mathchoice{i}{i}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle i$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i$}}},\psi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})=\int_{0}^{1}\psi_{\mathchoice{i}{i}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle i$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i$}}}(t)\psi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(t)\mathrm{d}t=(\phi_{\mathchoice{i}{i}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle i$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i$}}},\phi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{p}={\bf 1}_{\{i=j\}},

and the corresponding Fourier coefficients in
{\bf L}_{\mathchoice{2}{2}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}}[0,1]
can be represented
as

\bar{\theta}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}=(S,\psi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})=\theta_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}+h_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\,,

(3.6)

where

h_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}=h_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(S)=\sum_{k=1}^{p}\int_{t_{k-1}}^{t_{k}}\phi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(t_{k})\left(S(t)-S(t_{k})\right)\,\mathrm{d}t.

Therefore, we can represent the Fourier coefficients estimators as

\widehat{\theta}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}=\bar{\theta}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\xi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\xi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}I_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(\psi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})\,.

(3.7)

Through this representation we introduce the following condition for the noise process (
1.3
)
which will be used for the improved estimation below.

{\bf H}_{\mathchoice{2}{2}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}}
)
There exists
p_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}\geq 1
and
d_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}\geq 1
such that
for any
p\geq p_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}
and
d\geq d_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}
there exists a
\sigma
- field
{\cal G}

for which
the random variables

(\xi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{1\leq j\leq d}{1\leq j\leq d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq j\leq d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq j\leq d$}}}

are the
{\cal G}
-conditionally Gaussian with the covariance matrix

{\bf G}={\bf G}_{\mathchoice{Q}{Q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q$}}}=\left({\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q}{Q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q$}}}\,\xi_{\mathchoice{i}{i}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle i$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i$}}}\,\xi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}|{\cal G})\right)_{\mathchoice{1\leq i,j\leq d}{1\leq i,j\leq d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq i,j\leq d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq i,j\leq d$}}}

(3.8)

and for some nonrandom
constant
l_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}=l_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}(p,d)>0

\inf_{\mathchoice{Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}}\quad\left(\mbox{tr}\,{\bf G}-\,\lambda_{\mathchoice{max}{max}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle max$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle max$}}}({\bf G})\right)\geq l_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}\quad\mbox{a.s.}\,,

(3.9)

where
\mbox{tr}\,{\bf G}
is the trace and
\lambda_{\mathchoice{max}{max}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle max$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle max$}}}({\bf G})
is the maximal eigenvalue of the matrix
{\bf G}
.

Now we give an example
for this condition.

Proposition 3.1
.

The conditional
covariance matrix
(
3.8
)
constructed
for the model (
1.3
) – (
2.5
)
with respect to the
{\cal G}=\sigma\{z_{\mathchoice{t}{t}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t$}}}\,,\,t\geq 0\}

on the basis of the trigonometric functions (
3.3
)
for any odd
p\geq 3

and the distribution family
(
2.7
) – (
2.8
)
satisfies condition
{\bf H}_{\mathchoice{2}{2}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}})

with
l_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}=\underline{\varrho}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(d-6)/2

and

d_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}=\inf\left\{d\geq 7\,:\,5+\ln d\leq\check{a}d\right\}\,,\qquad\check{a}=\frac{1-e^{-a_{\mathchoice{max}{max}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle max$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle max$}}}}}{4a_{\mathchoice{max}{max}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle max$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle max$}}}}\,.

(3.10)

Given condition
{\bf H}_{\mathchoice{2}{2}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}})

for the first
d
Fourier coefficients in (
3.7
),
we will use
the improved estimation method
proposed
for parametric models
in

[
31
]
. We
define

\theta^{*}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}=\left(1-g(j)\right)\,\widehat{\theta}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad g(j)=\frac{{\bf c}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{|\widehat{\theta}|_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}}\,{\bf 1}_{\mathchoice{\{1\leq j\leq d\}}{\{1\leq j\leq d\}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\{1\leq j\leq d\}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\{1\leq j\leq d\}$}}}\,,

(3.11)

where
\widehat{\theta}=(\widehat{\theta}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{1\leq j\leq p}{1\leq j\leq p}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq j\leq p$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq j\leq p$}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
and
the norm
|x|^{2}_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}=\sum^{d}_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}\,x^{2}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}

for any vector
x=(x_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{1\leq j\leq p}{1\leq j\leq p}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq j\leq p$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq j\leq p$}}}
from
{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
with
p\geq d
. Here

{\bf c}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}=\frac{l_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}{n\left(r+\sqrt{\bar{d}\varkappa^{*}}\right)}\,,\qquad\bar{d}=\frac{d}{n}\,,

(3.12)

and the coefficient
\varkappa^{*}
is defined in condition
{\bf H}_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}})
.
The positive parameter

r
may be dependent of
n
, i.e.
r=r_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}
, and such that

\lim_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}\,n^{-\epsilon}\,r_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}=0\quad\mbox{for any}\quad\epsilon>0\,.

(3.13)

To compare the estimators
(
3.5
) and (
3.11
)
we put

\Delta_{\mathchoice{Q,S}{Q,S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,S$}}}:={\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q,S}{Q,S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,S$}}}\,|\theta^{*}-\theta|^{2}_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}-{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q,S}{Q,S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,S$}}}\,|\widehat{\theta}-\theta|^{2}_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}\,,

(3.14)

where
\theta^{*}=(\theta^{*}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{1\leq j\leq p}{1\leq j\leq p}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq j\leq p$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq j\leq p$}}}
and
\widehat{\theta}=(\widehat{\theta}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{1\leq j\leq p}{1\leq j\leq p}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq j\leq p$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq j\leq p$}}}
are the vectors in
{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
.
Now we study this difference.

Theorem 3.2
.

Assume that the function
S
is Lipschitzian. Then for any

n\geq 1
,
d\geq 2
and
p\geq 1
for which conditions
{\bf H}_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}})
–
{\bf H}_{\mathchoice{2}{2}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}})
hold

\Delta^{*}=\sup_{Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}\,\sup_{\mathchoice{\|S\|\leq r}{\|S\|\leq r}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\|S\|\leq r$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\|S\|\leq r$}}}\Delta_{\mathchoice{Q,S}{Q,S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,S$}}}\leq-{\bf c}^{2}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}+\frac{2\sqrt{d}\phi_{*}L}{p}{\bf c}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\,,

(3.15)

where

L=\sup_{\mathchoice{0\leq s,t\leq 1}{0\leq s,t\leq 1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0\leq s,t\leq 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0\leq s,t\leq 1$}}}\frac{|S(t)-S(s)|}{|t-s|}\,.

Proof.
First note that

{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q,S}{Q,S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,S$}}}|\theta^{*}-\bar{\theta}|_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}^{2}={\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q,S}{Q,S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,S$}}}|\widehat{\theta}-\bar{\theta}|_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}^{2}+{\bf c}^{2}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}-2{\bf c}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q,S}{Q,S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,S$}}}\,\sum^{d}_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}J_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\,,

where
\bar{\theta}=(\bar{\theta}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{1\leq j\leq p}{1\leq j\leq p}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq j\leq p$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq j\leq p$}}}
,

J_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}={\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q,S}{Q,S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,S$}}}\left(\iota_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(\widehat{\theta})(\widehat{\theta}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}-\bar{\theta}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})|{\cal G}\right)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\iota_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(x)=\frac{x_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}}{|x|_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}}\,.

In view of condition
{\bf H}_{\mathchoice{2}{2}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}})
the vector
(\widehat{\theta}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{1\leq j\leq d}{1\leq j\leq d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq j\leq d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq j\leq d$}}}

is the
{\cal G}
-conditionally Gaussian vector in
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
with mean vector
{\bf m}=(\bar{\theta}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{1\leq j\leq d}{1\leq j\leq d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq j\leq d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq j\leq d$}}}

and covariance matrix
n^{-1}{\bf G}
, then we obtain

J_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\,\iota_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(x)(x_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}-\bar{\theta}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}){\bf p}(x|{\cal G})\mathrm{d}x

with

{\bf p}(x|{\cal G})=\frac{n^{d/2}}{(2\pi)^{d/2}\sqrt{\det({\bf G})}}\exp\left(-\frac{n(x-{\bf m})^{\prime}\,{\bf G}^{-1}(x-{\bf m})}{2}\right)\,.

Changing the variables by

x={\bf s}(u)={\bf G}^{1/2}u+{\bf m}

and denoting by

{\bf v}_{\mathchoice{ij}{ij}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle ij$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle ij$}}}
the
(i,j)
-th element of
{\bf G}^{1/2}
,
we get

J_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}=\frac{n^{d/2}}{(2\pi)^{d/2}}\sum_{l=1}^{d}{\bf v}_{\mathchoice{j,l}{j,l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,l$}}}\,\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\widetilde{\iota}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(u)u_{l}\exp\left(-\frac{n|u|^{2}_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}}{2}\right)\mathrm{d}u\,,

where

\widetilde{\iota}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(u)=\iota_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}({\bf s}(u))
.
Furthermore, the integrating by parts over the variable
u_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}

implies that

\frac{n^{d/2}}{(2\pi)^{d/2}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\widetilde{\iota}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(u)u_{l}\exp\left(-\frac{n|u|^{2}_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}}{2}\right)\mathrm{d}u=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\mathchoice{k=1}{k=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k=1$}}}^{d}\,{\bf v}_{\mathchoice{k,l}{k,l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,l$}}}\,\int_{\mathchoice{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathbb{R}}^{d}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathbb{R}}^{d}$}}}\,H_{\mathchoice{j,k}{j,k}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,k$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,k$}}}({\bf s}(u)){\bf p}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}(u)\mathrm{d}u

where
H_{\mathchoice{j,k}{j,k}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,k$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,k$}}}(z)=\partial\iota_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(z)/\partial z_{\mathchoice{k}{k}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}
,

{\bf p}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}
is the Gaussian density in
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
with the parameters
(0,n^{-1}I_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}})
and
I_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}
is the identity matrix of order
d
.
Taking into account that

H_{\mathchoice{j,k}{j,k}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,k$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,k$}}}(z)=\frac{|z|^{2}_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}{\bf 1}_{\mathchoice{\{k=j\}}{\{k=j\}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\{k=j\}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\{k=j\}$}}}-z_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}z_{\mathchoice{k}{k}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}{|z|^{3/2}_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}}

and
{\bf g}_{\mathchoice{j,k}{j,k}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,k$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,k$}}}=\sum_{l=1}^{d}\sum_{\mathchoice{k=1}{k=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k=1$}}}^{d}\,{\bf v}_{\mathchoice{j,l}{j,l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,l$}}}\,{\bf v}_{\mathchoice{k,l}{k,l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,l$}}}
is the
(j,k)
-th element of the matrix
{\bf G}
,
we find

J_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}={\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q,S}{Q,S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,S$}}}\left(\frac{{\bf g}_{\mathchoice{j,j}{j,j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,j$}}}}{|\widehat{\theta}|_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}}-\frac{\sum^{d}_{\mathchoice{k=1}{k=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k=1$}}}{\bf g}_{\mathchoice{j,k}{j,k}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,k$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,k$}}}\widehat{\theta}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\widehat{\theta}_{\mathchoice{k}{k}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}{|\widehat{\theta}|^{3/2}_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}}|{\cal G}\right)\,.

In view of the inequality

\sum^{d}_{\mathchoice{k,j=1}{k,j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,j=1$}}}{\bf g}_{\mathchoice{j,k}{j,k}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,k$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,k$}}}\widehat{\theta}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\widehat{\theta}_{\mathchoice{k}{k}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}\leq\lambda_{max}({\bf G})|\widehat{\theta}|^{2}_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}

and condition
{\bf H}_{\mathchoice{2}{2}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}})

we have

\displaystyle{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q,S}{Q,S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,S$}}}|\theta^{*}-\bar{\theta}|_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}^{2}

\displaystyle-{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q,S}{Q,S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,S$}}}|\widehat{\theta}-\bar{\theta}|_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}^{2}={\bf c}^{2}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}-2{\bf c}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}n^{-1}{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q,S}{Q,S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,S$}}}\,\left(\frac{\mbox{tr}{\bf G}}{|\widehat{\theta}|_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}}-\frac{\sum^{d}_{\mathchoice{k,j=1}{k,j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,j=1$}}}{\bf g}_{\mathchoice{j,k}{j,k}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,k$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,k$}}}\widehat{\theta}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\widehat{\theta}_{\mathchoice{k}{k}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}{|\widehat{\theta}|_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}^{3}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq{\bf c}^{2}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}-\frac{2{\bf c}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\,l_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}{n}\,{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q,S}{Q,S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,S$}}}\,\frac{1}{|\widehat{\theta}|_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}}\,.

Besides, using the Jensen inequality, we can estimate the last expectation from below as

{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q,S}{Q,S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,S$}}}\,\frac{1}{|\widehat{\theta}|_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}}={\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q,S}{Q,S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,S$}}}\,\frac{1}{|\bar{\theta}+n^{-1/2}\xi|_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}}\geq\frac{1}{|\bar{\theta}|_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}+n^{-1/2}{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q,S}{Q,S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,S$}}}|\xi|_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}}\,,

where the vector
\xi=(\xi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{1\leq j\leq p}{1\leq j\leq p}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq j\leq p$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq j\leq p$}}}
and its components are defined in (
3.7
).
From (
2.2
)
we get

{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q,S}{Q,S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,S$}}}|\xi|^{2}_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}=\sum^{d}_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}\,{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q,S}{Q,S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,S$}}}\,\xi^{2}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\leq\frac{\varkappa_{\mathchoice{Q}{Q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q$}}}}{n}\sum^{d}_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}\int^{n}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}\phi^{2}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\mathrm{d}t\leq\varkappa^{*}\,d\,.

Since
|\bar{\theta}|_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}\leq\|S\|
, then for
\|S\|\leq r

{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q,S}{Q,S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,S$}}}\,|\widehat{\theta}|^{-1}_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}\geq\left(r+\sqrt{d\varkappa^{*}/n}\right)^{-1}=\frac{1}{r+\sqrt{\bar{d}\varkappa^{*}}}

and, therefore,

\displaystyle{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q,S}{Q,S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,S$}}}\,|\theta^{*}-\bar{\theta}|_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}^{2}\,

\displaystyle\leq{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q,S}{Q,S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,S$}}}|\widehat{\theta}-\bar{\theta}|_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}^{2}+{\bf c}^{2}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}-\frac{2{\bf c}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\,(d-1)l_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}{n(r+\sqrt{\bar{d}\varkappa^{*}})}

\displaystyle={\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q,S}{Q,S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,S$}}}|\widehat{\theta}-\bar{\theta}|_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}^{2}-{\bf c}^{2}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\,.

(3.16)

The applying here the representation (
3.6
) implies

\displaystyle{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q,S}{Q,S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,S$}}}\,|\theta^{*}-\bar{\theta}|_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}^{2}

\displaystyle={\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q,S}{Q,S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,S$}}}\,|\theta^{*}-\theta|_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}^{2}-2\sum^{d}_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}\,{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q,S}{Q,S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,S$}}}(\theta^{*}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}-\theta_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})h_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}+|h|^{2}_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}

\displaystyle\leq{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q,S}{Q,S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,S$}}}|\widehat{\theta}-\theta|_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}^{2}-{\bf c}^{2}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}-2\sum^{d}_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}\,{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q,S}{Q,S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,S$}}}(\widehat{\theta}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}-\theta_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})h_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}+|h|^{2}_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}\,.

So, the risks difference
(
3.14
) can be estimated from above as

\Delta_{\mathchoice{Q,S}{Q,S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,S$}}}\leq-{\bf c}^{2}_{n}+2\sum^{d}_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}\,{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q,S}{Q,S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,S$}}}(\theta^{*}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}-\widehat{\theta}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})h_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\,.

(3.17)

Taking into account that for any
\varepsilon>0

2|ab|\leq\varepsilon a^{2}+\varepsilon^{-1}b^{2}\,,

we obtain

2\sum^{d}_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}\,{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q,S}{Q,S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,S$}}}(\theta^{*}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}-\widehat{\theta}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})h_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\leq\varepsilon{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q,S}{Q,S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,S$}}}|\theta^{*}-\widehat{\theta}|_{d}^{2}+\varepsilon^{-1}|h|_{d}^{2}=\varepsilon{\bf c}^{2}_{n}+\varepsilon^{-1}|h|_{d}^{2}\,.

Now, from definition of the coefficients
h_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}
in (
3.6
) and by the Cauchy-Bunyakovsky-Schwarz inequality, we have the estimate

\displaystyle|h|_{d}^{2}\,

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{p}\int_{t_{k-1}}^{t_{k}}\phi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(t_{k})\left(S(t)-S(t_{k})\right)dt\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\,\leq\sum_{j=1}^{d}\sum_{k=1}^{p}\int_{t_{k-1}}^{t_{k}}\phi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}^{2}(t_{k})\mathrm{d}t\,\sum_{k=1}^{p}\int_{t_{k-1}}^{t_{k}}\left(S(t)-S(t_{k})\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}t\leq\frac{d\phi_{*}^{2}L^{2}}{p^{2}}\,.

Using this in
(
3.17
), we get

\Delta_{\mathchoice{Q,S}{Q,S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,S$}}}\leq-(1-\varepsilon){\bf c}^{2}_{n}+\varepsilon^{-1}\frac{d\phi_{*}^{2}L^{2}}{p^{2}}\,.

Minimizing this upper bound over
\varepsilon>0
, we come to the inequality (
3.15
). Hence
Theorem
3.2
.

∎

Let now

p_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}=\frac{2\sqrt{d}\phi_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}L}{{\bf c}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}=\frac{2\phi_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}L(r+\sqrt{\bar{d}\varkappa^{*}})n\sqrt{d}}{l_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}\,,

(3.18)

where
L
is the Lipschitz constant for
S
defined in (
3.15
).

Remark 3.1
.

Note that uniformly over
p
for
p>p_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}
the upper bound in (
3.15
) is negative, i.e.
\Delta^{*}<0
.
This means that non-asymptotically, i.e. for the bounded number observations
n
,
the estimate (
3.11
) outperforms in mean square accuracy the estimate (
3.5
).

Remark 3.2
.

For the model (
1.3
) – (
2.5
)
with
\underline{\varrho}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\equiv\underline{\varrho}_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}>0

asymptotically as
n,d\to\infty

for

p\geq 2p_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}\approx\frac{n}{\sqrt{d}}

(3.19)

we obtain that

\Delta^{*}\leq-\frac{\underline{\varrho}^{2}_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}(d-6)^{2}}{2\left(r+\sqrt{\bar{d}\varkappa^{*}}\right)^{2}\,n^{2}}\approx-\left(\frac{d}{n}\right)^{2}\,.

We recall that in the parametric case the upper bound in (
3.15
)
asymptotically goes to zero as
n^{-2}
(see,
[
27
,
31
]
) since the parametric dimension is fixed, but in this case
the dimension
d\to\infty
. Therefore, Theorem
3.2
implies that for the nonparametric models
with incomplete observations
the improvement effect is more significant than in the parametric models uniformly over
observation frequency.

4
Improved model selection

In this section we construct model selection procedures
based on the improved estimators (
3.11
). To this end, by the same way as
in
[
26
]
we define a class of weighted least squares estimates

(S^{*}_{\mathchoice{\gamma}{\gamma}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\gamma$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\gamma$}}})_{\mathchoice{\gamma\in\Gamma}{\gamma\in\Gamma}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\gamma\in\Gamma$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\gamma\in\Gamma$}}}
as

\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{\gamma}{\gamma}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\gamma$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\gamma$}}}(t)=\sum^{p}_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}\gamma(j)\widehat{\theta}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\psi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(t)\,,

(4.1)

where
\widehat{\theta}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}
is defined in (
3.7
), the weights
\gamma=(\gamma(j))_{1\leq j\leq p}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
belong to some
finite set
\Gamma\subset[0,1]^{p}
. For this set we denote

\nu=\#(\Gamma)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\nu_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}=\max_{\gamma\in\Gamma}\,\sum_{j=1}^{p}\gamma(j)\,,

(4.2)

where
\#(\Gamma)
is the number of the vectors
\gamma
in
\Gamma
.
In the sequel we assume that all vectors from
\Gamma
satisfies the following condition.

{\bf H}_{\mathchoice{3}{3}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 3$}}}
)
Assume that the set
\Gamma
is such for any vector
\gamma\in\Gamma
there exists
some fixed integer
d=d(\gamma)\leq p

such that their first
d
components
are equal to one, i.e.
\gamma(j)=1
for
1\leq j\leq d
for any
\gamma\in\Gamma
. Moreover, we assume that
the parameters
\nu
and
\nu_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}
are functions of
n
, i.e.
\nu=\nu(n)
and
\nu_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}=\nu_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}(n)
,
such that for any
\epsilon>0

\lim_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}\frac{\nu}{n^{\epsilon}}=0\quad\mbox{and}\quad\lim_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}\frac{\nu_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}}{n^{1/3+\epsilon}}=0\,.

(4.3)

Now we define shrinkage estimates for
S

S^{*}_{\mathchoice{\gamma}{\gamma}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\gamma$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\gamma$}}}(t)=\sum^{p}_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}\gamma(j)\theta^{*}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\psi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(t)\,,

(4.4)

where the shrinkage estimators
\theta^{*}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}
are defined in (
3.11
)
with the parameter
d
given in condition
{\bf H}_{\mathchoice{3}{3}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 3$}}}
). In this case the threshold
{\bf c}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}

is the function of
\gamma
also, i.e.
{\bf c}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}={\bf c}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(\gamma)
. In the sequel we will use the maximum of this
sequence, i.e.

{\bf c}^{*}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}=n\,\max_{\mathchoice{\gamma\in\Gamma}{\gamma\in\Gamma}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\gamma\in\Gamma$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\gamma\in\Gamma$}}}\,{\bf c}^{2}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(\gamma)\,.

(4.5)
