{\bf H}_{\mathchoice{4}{4}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 4$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 4$}}}
)
Assume that
\lim_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}n^{-\epsilon}{\bf c}^{*}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}=0
for any
\epsilon>0
.

Now
we compare the estimators (
4.1
) and (
4.4
).

Theorem 4.1
.

Assume that the function
S
is Lipschitzian. Then for any

n\geq 3
,
d\geq 2
,
p\geq 1
and
\gamma\in\Gamma

for which the conditions
{\bf H}_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}})
–
{\bf H}_{\mathchoice{3}{3}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 3$}}})
hold

\sup_{\mathchoice{Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}}\,\sup_{\mathchoice{\|S\|\leq r}{\|S\|\leq r}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\|S\|\leq r$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\|S\|\leq r$}}}\left({\cal R}_{\mathchoice{Q}{Q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q$}}}(S^{*}_{\mathchoice{\gamma}{\gamma}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\gamma$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\gamma$}}},S)-{\cal R}_{\mathchoice{Q}{Q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q$}}}(\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{\gamma}{\gamma}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\gamma$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\gamma$}}},S)\right)\leq-{\bf c}^{2}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\,,

(4.6)

where the coefficient
{\bf c}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}
is defined in
(
3.12
).

Proof.
First, introducing the projection

\bar{S}=\sum^{p}_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}\bar{\theta}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\psi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(t)\,,

(4.7)

we have

\|S^{*}_{\mathchoice{\gamma}{\gamma}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\gamma$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\gamma$}}}-S\|^{2}=\|S^{*}_{\mathchoice{\gamma}{\gamma}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\gamma$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\gamma$}}}-\bar{S}\|^{2}+\|S-\bar{S}\|^{2}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\|\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{\gamma}{\gamma}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\gamma$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\gamma$}}}-S\|^{2}=\|\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{\gamma}{\gamma}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\gamma$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\gamma$}}}-\bar{S}\|^{2}+\|S-\bar{S}\|^{2}\,.

Therefore, by condition
{\bf H}_{\mathchoice{3}{3}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 3$}}})
, we get

{\cal R}_{\mathchoice{Q}{Q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q$}}}(S^{*}_{\mathchoice{\gamma}{\gamma}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\gamma$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\gamma$}}},S)-{\cal R}_{\mathchoice{Q}{Q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q$}}}(\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{\gamma}{\gamma}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\gamma$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\gamma$}}},S)={\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q,S}{Q,S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,S$}}}\,|\theta^{*}-\bar{\theta}|_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}^{2}-{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q,S}{Q,S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,S$}}}|\widehat{\theta}-\bar{\theta}|_{\mathchoice{d}{d}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle d$}}}^{2}\,.

Using the inequality (
3.16
), we obtain the bound (
4.6
).
Hence Theorem
4.1
.

∎

Remark 4.1
.

We would like to emphasize that the accuracy improvement provided by the upper bound (
4.6
) for
the weighted least squares estimate (
4.4
)
is uniform over the observation frequency
p\geq 1
. Indeed, we obtained the same upper bound as for the
estimation problem on the complete observations (see, Theorem 3.1 in
[
36
]
).
This is the new
effect in the improvement nonparametric estimation theory.

To construct model selection procedures
we need to impose some stability conditions
introduced in
[
34
]

for the noise sequence in
(
3.7
).

{\bf C}_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}})

There exists a proxy variance

\sigma_{\mathchoice{Q}{Q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q$}}}>0

such that for any
\epsilon>0

\lim_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}\frac{1}{n^{\epsilon}}\,\sup_{\mathchoice{Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}}\,\sum^{p}_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}\,\left|{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q}{Q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q$}}}\,\xi^{2}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}-\sigma_{\mathchoice{Q}{Q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q$}}}\right|=0\,.

Further we will need the following function

{\bf L}_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}}(Q)=\sum^{p}_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}\,\left|{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q}{Q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q$}}}\,\xi^{2}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}-\sigma_{\mathchoice{Q}{Q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q$}}}\right|\,.

(4.8)

To study the total noise variance in (
3.7
) we denote

{\bf L}_{2}(Q)=\sup_{\mathchoice{x\in{\cal B}_{\mathchoice{p}{p}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}}}}{x\in{\cal B}_{\mathchoice{p}{p}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle x\in{\cal B}_{\mathchoice{p}{p}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x\in{\cal B}_{\mathchoice{p}{p}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}}}$}}}{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q}{Q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q$}}}\,\left(\sum^{p}_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}\,x_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\,\widetilde{\xi}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\right)^{2}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\widetilde{\xi}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}=\xi^{2}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}-{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q}{Q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q$}}}\xi^{2}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\,,

(4.9)

where
{\cal B}_{\mathchoice{p}{p}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}}}=\{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}\,:\,|x|_{\mathchoice{p}{p}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}}}\leq 1\}
and

|\cdot|_{\mathchoice{p}{p}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}}}
is Euclidean norm in
{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
.

{\bf C}_{\mathchoice{2}{2}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}})

Assume that the sequence

{\bf L}^{*}_{\mathchoice{2,n}{2,n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2,n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2,n$}}}=\sup_{\mathchoice{Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}}{\bf L}_{2}(Q)
is such that

\lim_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}\frac{{\bf L}^{*}_{\mathchoice{2,n}{2,n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2,n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2,n$}}}}{n^{\epsilon}}=0\quad\mbox{for any}\quad\epsilon>0\,.

As is shown in Propositions
7.1
-
7.2
, the
trigonometric basis (
3.3
) provides the both conditions
{\bf C}_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}})

and
{\bf C}_{\mathchoice{2}{2}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}})
for the
noise process (
2.5
)
with the properties (
2.7
) – (
2.8
).

To choose a weight vector
\gamma
in
\Gamma
we use the quadratic estimation accuracy

\mbox{Err}\,(\gamma)=\|S^{*}_{\mathchoice{\gamma}{\gamma}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\gamma$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\gamma$}}}-S\|^{2}.

By the definition
(
4.4
) and the projection (
4.7
) we can represent this accuracy as

\displaystyle\mbox{Err}(\gamma)

\displaystyle=\sum^{p}_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}(\gamma(j)\theta^{*}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}-\bar{\theta}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})^{2}+\|S-\bar{S}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum^{p}_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}\,\gamma^{2}(j)(\theta^{*}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})^{2}\,-2\,\sum^{p}_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}\,\gamma(j)\theta^{*}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\,\bar{\theta}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\,+\,\|S\|^{2}\,.

(4.10)

It is clear, that to choose a good weighted vector
one needs to minimize this function over
\gamma

in
\Gamma
. The coefficients

(\bar{\theta}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{1\leq j\leq p}{1\leq j\leq p}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq j\leq p$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq j\leq p$}}}

are unknown, and we need to replace in this function the terms

\theta^{*}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\,\bar{\theta}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}
by their estimators.
Similar to

[
26
]

in this case we use the estimators defined as

\widetilde{\theta}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}=\theta^{*}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\,\widehat{\theta}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}-\frac{\widehat{\sigma}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{n}\,,

(4.11)

where
\widehat{\sigma}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}
is an estimate
for the proxy variance
\sigma_{\mathchoice{Q}{Q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q$}}}
defined in the condition
{\bf C}_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}})
.
As in
[
26
]

we define it
through the estimators of the Fourier coefficients on the
trigonometric basis (
3.3
), i.e.

\widehat{\sigma}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}=\frac{n}{p}\sum_{\mathchoice{j=[\sqrt{n}]+1}{j=[\sqrt{n}]+1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=[\sqrt{n}]+1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=[\sqrt{n}]+1$}}}^{n}\widehat{{\bf t}}^{2}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\widehat{{\bf t}}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}=\frac{1}{n}\sum^{np}_{\mathchoice{l=1}{l=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l=1$}}}\,\mbox{Tr}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(t_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}})\,(y_{\mathchoice{t_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}}{t_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}$}}}-y_{\mathchoice{t_{\mathchoice{l-1}{l-1}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l-1$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l-1$}}}}{t_{\mathchoice{l-1}{l-1}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l-1$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l-1$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t_{\mathchoice{l-1}{l-1}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l-1$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l-1$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t_{\mathchoice{l-1}{l-1}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l-1$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l-1$}}}$}}})\,.

(4.12)

According to the model selection approach for the dependent observations
(see, for example, in
[
12
,
26
]
),
when we modify the quadratic accuracy
one needs to penalize this modification by adding in (
4.10
) a special positive penalty term.
In this case the penalization means that the additional positive term makes the minimization problem more difficult. So, for any
\gamma\in\Gamma

we set the objective function as

J(\gamma)\,=\,\sum^{p}_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}\,\gamma^{2}(j)(\theta^{*}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})^{2}\,-2\,\sum^{p}_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}\,\gamma(j)\,\widetilde{\theta}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\,+\,\rho\,\widehat{P}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(\gamma)\,,

(4.13)

where
\rho
is some positive penalization level,

\widehat{P}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(\gamma)
is the penalty term defined as

\widehat{P}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(\gamma)=\frac{\widehat{\sigma}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\,|\gamma|^{2}_{\mathchoice{p}{p}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}}}}{n}\,.

(4.14)

In the case, when the value of
\sigma_{\mathchoice{Q}{Q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q$}}}
in
{\bf C}_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}})
is known, we have

\widehat{\sigma}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}=\sigma_{\mathchoice{Q}{Q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q$}}}
and

P_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(\gamma)=\frac{\sigma_{\mathchoice{Q}{Q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q$}}}\,|\gamma|^{2}_{\mathchoice{p}{p}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}}}}{n}\,.

(4.15)

Finally, we define the improved model selection procedure as

\gamma^{*}=\mbox{argmin}_{\mathchoice{\gamma\in\Gamma}{\gamma\in\Gamma}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\gamma\in\Gamma$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\gamma\in\Gamma$}}}\,J(\gamma)\quad\mbox{and}\quad S^{*}=S^{*}_{\mathchoice{\gamma^{*}}{\gamma^{*}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\gamma^{*}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\gamma^{*}$}}}\,.

(4.16)

It will be noted that
\gamma^{*}
exists because

\Gamma
is a finite set. If the
minimizing sequence in (
4.16
)
\gamma^{*}
is not
unique, one can take any minimizer.

{\bf C}_{\mathchoice{3}{3}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 3$}}}
)
Assume that for any
n\geq 1

\varsigma^{*}=\varsigma^{*}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}=\sup_{\mathchoice{Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}}\sigma_{\mathchoice{Q}{Q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q$}}}<\infty\quad\mbox{and}\quad\varsigma_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}=\varsigma_{\mathchoice{*,n}{*,n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*,n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*,n$}}}=\inf_{\mathchoice{Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}}\sigma_{\mathchoice{Q}{Q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q$}}}>0

(4.17)

and for any
\epsilon>0
the ratio
\varsigma^{*}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}/n^{\epsilon}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
.

We use this condition to construct the special set
\Gamma
of weight vectors
(\gamma(j))_{\mathchoice{j\geq 1}{j\geq 1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j\geq 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j\geq 1$}}}
as it is proposed in
[
12
,
13
]

for which we will study the asymptotic properties of the model selection procedure (
4.16
).
For this we consider the following grid

{\cal A}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}=\{1,\ldots,k^{*}\}\times\{r_{1},\ldots,r_{m}\}\,,

where
r_{i}=i\varepsilon
,
i=\overline{1,\,m}
with
m=[1/\varepsilon^{2}]
. We assume that the
parameters
k^{*}\geq 1
and
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
are
functions of
n
, i.e.
k^{*}=k^{*}(n)
and

\varepsilon=\varepsilon(n)
, such that

\lim_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}\,\left(\frac{1}{k^{*}(n)}+\frac{k^{*}(n)}{\ln n}\right)=0\quad\mbox{and}\quad\lim_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}\,\left(\varepsilon(n)+\frac{1}{n^{{\bf b}}\varepsilon(n)}\right)=0

(4.18)

for any
{\bf b}>0
. One can take, for example, for
0<\varepsilon<1

\varepsilon(n)=1/\ln(n+1)\quad\mbox{and}\quad k^{*}(n)=k^{*}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}+\sqrt{\ln(n+1)}\,,

(4.19)

where
k^{*}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}\geq 0
is some fixed constant.
For each
\alpha=(\beta,r)\in{\cal A}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}
we introduce the weight
sequence
\gamma_{\mathchoice{\alpha}{\alpha}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\alpha$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha$}}}=(\gamma_{\mathchoice{\alpha}{\alpha}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\alpha$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha$}}}(j))_{\mathchoice{j\geq 1}{j\geq 1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j\geq 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j\geq 1$}}}

as

\gamma_{\mathchoice{\alpha}{\alpha}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\alpha$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha$}}}(j)={\bf 1}_{\mathchoice{\{1\leq j\leq j_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}(\alpha)\}}{\{1\leq j\leq j_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}(\alpha)\}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\{1\leq j\leq j_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}(\alpha)\}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\{1\leq j\leq j_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}(\alpha)\}$}}}+\left(1-(j/\omega_{\alpha})^{\beta}\right)\,{\bf 1}_{\mathchoice{\{j_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}(\alpha)<j\leq\omega_{\alpha}\}}{\{j_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}(\alpha)<j\leq\omega_{\alpha}\}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\{j_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}(\alpha)<j\leq\omega_{\alpha}\}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\{j_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}(\alpha)<j\leq\omega_{\alpha}\}$}}}\,,

(4.20)

where
j_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}(\alpha)=\omega_{\mathchoice{\alpha}{\alpha}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\alpha$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha$}}}/\ln(n+1)
,

\omega_{\mathchoice{\alpha}{\alpha}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\alpha$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha$}}}=\left(\frac{(\beta+1)(2\beta+1)}{\pi^{2\beta}\beta}\,r\,v_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\right)^{1/(2\beta+1)}\quad\mbox{and}\quad v_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}=n/\varsigma^{*}\,.

Finally, we set

\Gamma\,=\,\{\gamma_{\mathchoice{\alpha}{\alpha}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\alpha$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha$}}}\,,\,\alpha\in{\cal A}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\}\,.

(4.21)

As we will see later, the model selection procedure (
4.16
)
with this weight set will be efficient in adaptive setting.

Remark 4.2
.

As it is shown in
[
26
]

the weight coefficients (
4.21
) satisfy condition
{\bf H}_{\mathchoice{3}{3}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 3$}}})

with
d=[j_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}(\alpha)]
. Moreover, for the model (
1.3
) – (
2.5
) the using of the trigonometric basis (
3.3
) and
Proposition
3.1
provide condition
{\bf H}_{\mathchoice{4}{4}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 4$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 4$}}})
with the family
(
4.21
).

Remark 4.3
.

As it is shown in
[
38
]
in the case when the regularity parameters
of the function
S
are known,
the weighted least squares estimate
(
4.1
) with the weights of the form (
4.20
) is asymptotically efficient. In this paper
we use these weights to provide through Theorem
4.1
the efficient property for the improved model selection procedure
(
4.16
) in the adaptive case, i.e. when the regularity properties are unknown.

5
Main results

In this Section we obtain
the sharp oracle inequalities for the quadratic risk (
2.3
) and robust risk (
2.4
) of proposed procedure.
Then, on the basis of these inequalities, we will establish the robust efficiency property in adaptive setting.

5.1
Oracle inequalities

First, we obtain the oracle inequalities for the risks (
2.3
).

Theorem 5.1
.

Assume that conditions
{\bf C}_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}})
-
{\bf C}_{\mathchoice{3}{3}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 3$}}})
hold. Then, for any
n\geq 3
,
p\geq 1

and the weight family
\Gamma
for which conditions
{\bf H}_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}})
-
{\bf H}_{\mathchoice{4}{4}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 4$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 4$}}})
hold also
and for any
0<\rho<1/2

{\cal R}_{\mathchoice{Q}{Q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q$}}}(S^{*},S)\,\leq\,\frac{1+5\rho}{1-\rho}\min_{\mathchoice{\gamma\in\Gamma}{\gamma\in\Gamma}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\gamma\in\Gamma$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\gamma\in\Gamma$}}}{\cal R}_{\mathchoice{Q}{Q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q$}}}(S^{*}_{\mathchoice{\gamma}{\gamma}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\gamma$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\gamma$}}},S)+\frac{{\bf U}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\left(1+\nu_{\mathchoice{*}{*}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle*$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle*$}}}{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q,S}{Q,S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,S$}}}|\widehat{\sigma}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}-\sigma_{\mathchoice{Q}{Q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q$}}}|\right)}{\rho n}\,,

(5.1)

where
the coefficient
{\bf U}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}
is such that

\lim_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}\frac{{\bf U}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{n^{\epsilon}}=0\quad\mbox{for any}\quad\epsilon>0\,.

(5.2)

Now we study the estimate (
4.12
). We need the following condition for the observation
frequency.

{\bf D})
Assume that the observation frequency
p
is a function of
n
, i.e.
p=p(n)
such that

1\leq p\leq n
and

\lim_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}n^{\epsilon-5/6}p=\infty
for any
\epsilon>0
.

Proposition 5.2
.

Assume that the conditions

{\bf C}_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}})
–
{\bf C}_{\mathchoice{3}{3}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 3$}}})
hold for the trigonometric basis (
3.3
).
Moreover, assume also that the unknown function
S
has the square integrated derivative
\dot{S}
and the conditions
{\bf H}_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}})
and
{\bf D})
hold.
Then for
n\geq 3
and
\sqrt{n}<p\leq n

{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q,S}{Q,S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,S$}}}|\widehat{\sigma}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}-\sigma_{\mathchoice{Q}{Q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q$}}}|\leq{\bf K}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}n^{-1/3}\left(1+\|\dot{S}\|^{2}\right)\,,

(5.3)

where the term
{\bf K}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}>0
is such that

\lim_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}\,n^{-\epsilon}\,{\bf K}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}=0
for any
\epsilon>0
.

Theorem
5.1
and Proposition
5.2
imply the oracle inequalities for the robust risks (
2.4
).

Theorem 5.3
.

Assume that conditions
{\bf C}_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}})
-
{\bf C}_{\mathchoice{3}{3}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 3$}}})
,
{\bf D})
hold and the function
S
has the square integrable derivative
\dot{S}
. Then, for any
n\geq 3
,
p\geq 1

and the weight family
\Gamma
for which the conditions
{\bf H}_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}})
-
{\bf H}_{\mathchoice{4}{4}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 4$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 4$}}})
hold also
and for any
0<\rho<1/2

\displaystyle{\cal R}^{*}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(S^{*},S)\,\leq\,\frac{1+5\rho}{1-\rho}\min_{\mathchoice{\gamma\in\Gamma}{\gamma\in\Gamma}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\gamma\in\Gamma$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\gamma\in\Gamma$}}}{\cal R}_{n}^{*}(S^{*}_{\mathchoice{\gamma}{\gamma}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\gamma$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\gamma$}}},S)+\frac{1}{\rho n}\,{\bf U}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(1+\|\dot{S}\|^{2})\,,

where the term
{\bf U}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}
satisfies the property
(
5.2
).

Remark 5.1
.

Conditions

{\bf C}_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}})
–
{\bf C}_{\mathchoice{3}{3}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 3$}}})
are usually required to obtain nonasymptotic sharp oracle inequalities for the statistical models
with the dependent observations
through the methods developed in

[
12
,
22
]
.
Condition
{\bf D})
is basic to provide the property (
5.3
).
Moreover, conditions
{\bf H}_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}})
-
{\bf H}_{\mathchoice{4}{4}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 4$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 4$}}})
are used to study the properties of the shrinkage estimators
(
4.4
).

Remark 5.2
.

Main difference between the weight estimators (
4.1
) and (
4.4
)
is that generally the shrinkage estimators
\theta^{*}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}
depend on the weight vector
\gamma
. By this reason to show the inequality (
5.1
)
we can’t use directly
the method developed in
[
26
]
for the weighted least squares estimates (
4.1
). For such estimator one needs to develop
a new special analytical tool.

5.2
Asymptotic efficiency

In order to study the asymptotic efficiency we define the following functional Sobolev ball

W_{\mathchoice{k,{\bf r}}{k,{\bf r}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}}=\left\{f\in{\cal C}^{(k)}_{\mathchoice{per}{per}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle per$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle per$}}}[0,1]\,:\,\sum_{\mathchoice{j=0}{j=0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=0$}}}^{k}\,\|f^{(j)}\|^{2}\leq{\bf r}\right\}\,,

where
{\bf r}>0
and
k\geq 1
are
some unknown parameters,
{\cal C}^{k}_{\mathchoice{per}{per}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle per$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle per$}}}[0,1]
is the space of

k
times differentiable
1
-periodic
{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}
functions
such that for any
0\leq i\leq k-1
the periodic boundary conditions are satisfied, i.e.
f^{(i)}(0)=f^{(i)}(1)
.
To analyze the efficiency properties for the model selection procedure (
4.16
)
one needs to compare this estimator with all possible estimation methods. To this end
we denote by
\Xi_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}
the set of all estimators
\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}
based on the observations
(y_{\mathchoice{t}{t}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t$}}})_{\mathchoice{0\leq t\leq n}{0\leq t\leq n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0\leq t\leq n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0\leq t\leq n$}}}

i.e. any

\sigma\{y_{\mathchoice{t}{t}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t$}}}\,,\,0\leq t\leq n\}
measurable functions.
To obtain the lower bound for the risks we will use the process (
1.3
)
with
\xi_{\mathchoice{t}{t}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t$}}}=\varsigma^{*}w_{\mathchoice{t}{t}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t$}}}
, i.e. the white noise model with the intensity (
4.17
).
We denote its distribution by
Q^{*}
.

Theorem 5.4
.

Assume that
Q^{*}\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}
.
Then robust risks (
2.4
) are bounded from below in the following sense

\liminf_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}\,v_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}^{2k/(2k+1)}\inf_{\mathchoice{\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\in\Xi_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\in\Xi_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\in\Xi_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\in\Xi_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}}\,\sup_{\mathchoice{S\in W_{\mathchoice{k,{\bf r}}{k,{\bf r}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}}}{S\in W_{\mathchoice{k,{\bf r}}{k,{\bf r}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle S\in W_{\mathchoice{k,{\bf r}}{k,{\bf r}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle S\in W_{\mathchoice{k,{\bf r}}{k,{\bf r}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}}$}}}\,{\cal R}^{*}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}},S)\,\geq l_{\mathchoice{k}{k}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}({\bf r})\,,

(5.4)

where
v_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}=n/\varsigma^{*}
and
l_{\mathchoice{k}{k}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}({\bf r})\,=\,((2k+1){\bf r})^{1/(2k+1)}\,\left(k/(\pi(k+1))\right)^{2k/(2k+1)}
.

We show that this lower bound is sharp in the following sense. To provide the efficient property for the model selection procedure
(
4.16
) we choose the parameter
\rho
as a function of
n
, i.e.
\rho=\rho(n)
such that

\lim_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}\,\rho_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}=0\quad\mbox{and for any }\,\epsilon>0\qquad\lim_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}\,n^{\epsilon}\rho_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}=\infty\,.

(5.5)

Theorem 5.5
.

Assume that conditions
{\bf C}_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}})
-
{\bf C}_{\mathchoice{3}{3}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 3$}}})
,
{\bf D})
,
{\bf H}_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}})
-
{\bf H}_{\mathchoice{4}{4}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 4$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 4$}}})
hold.
Then robust risk of the model selection procedure
(
4.16
) constructed on the trigonometric basis functions (
3.3
)
with the weight coefficients (
4.21
) and the penalty threshold satisfying the
conditions (
5.5
) is bounded from above as

\limsup_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}\,v_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}^{2k/(2k+1)}\sup_{\mathchoice{S\in W_{\mathchoice{k,{\bf r}}{k,{\bf r}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}}}{S\in W_{\mathchoice{k,{\bf r}}{k,{\bf r}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle S\in W_{\mathchoice{k,{\bf r}}{k,{\bf r}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle S\in W_{\mathchoice{k,{\bf r}}{k,{\bf r}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}}$}}}\,{\cal R}^{*}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(S^{*},S)\,\leq l_{\mathchoice{k}{k}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}({\bf r})\,.

It is clear that these theorems
imply the following efficient property.

Theorem 5.6
.

Assume that the conditions of Theorems

5.4
and
5.5
hold.
Then the model selection procedure
(
4.16
) constructed on the trigonometric basis functions (
3.3
) with the weight coefficients (
4.21
)
and the penalty threshold satisfying the
conditions (
5.5
)
is asymptotically efficient, i.e.

\lim_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}\,v_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}^{2k/(2k+1)}\,\sup_{\mathchoice{S\in W_{\mathchoice{k,{\bf r}}{k,{\bf r}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}}}{S\in W_{\mathchoice{k,{\bf r}}{k,{\bf r}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle S\in W_{\mathchoice{k,{\bf r}}{k,{\bf r}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle S\in W_{\mathchoice{k,{\bf r}}{k,{\bf r}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}}$}}}\,{\cal R}^{*}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(S^{*},S)\,=l_{\mathchoice{k}{k}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}({\bf r})

and

\lim_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}\frac{\inf_{\mathchoice{\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\in\Sigma_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\in\Sigma_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\in\Sigma_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\in\Sigma_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}}\,\sup_{\mathchoice{S\in W_{\mathchoice{k,{\bf r}}{k,{\bf r}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}}}{S\in W_{\mathchoice{k,{\bf r}}{k,{\bf r}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle S\in W_{\mathchoice{k,{\bf r}}{k,{\bf r}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle S\in W_{\mathchoice{k,{\bf r}}{k,{\bf r}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}}$}}}\,{\cal R}^{*}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(\widehat{S}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}},S)}{\sup_{\mathchoice{S\in W_{\mathchoice{k,{\bf r}}{k,{\bf r}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}}}{S\in W_{\mathchoice{k,{\bf r}}{k,{\bf r}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle S\in W_{\mathchoice{k,{\bf r}}{k,{\bf r}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle S\in W_{\mathchoice{k,{\bf r}}{k,{\bf r}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}}$}}}\,{\cal R}^{*}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(S^{*},S)}=1\,.

Theorem
5.4
is shown by the same way as Theorem 1
in
[
23
]
.
Theorem
5.5
follows from Theorems
5.3
,
4.1
and Theorem 5.2
in
[
26
]
.

Remark 5.3
.

The level
l_{\mathchoice{k}{k}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}({\bf r})
determining the lower bound (
5.4
) is the well-known Pinsker constant, obtained in

[
38
]
for the filtration problem in the model (
1.3
) with the standard white noise
(\xi_{\mathchoice{t}{t}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t$}}})_{\mathchoice{t\geq 0}{t\geq 0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t\geq 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t\geq 0$}}}
that modeled by
the standard Brownian motion. For the models with general semimartingale noise the lower bound is the same as for
the white noise model, but generally the convergence rate is not the same. In this case the convergence rate is given by

\left(n/\varsigma^{*}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\right)^{-2k/(2k+1)}
while in classical white noise model the convergence rate is
\left(n\right)^{-2k/(2k+1)}
.
So, if the upper variance threshold
\varsigma^{*}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}
tends to zero, the convergence rate is better than the classical one; if it tends to infinity, it is worse
and, if it is a constant, the rate is the same.

5.3
Statistical analysis for the big data model (
1.1
)

Now we apply our results for the high dimensional model (
1.1
). We assume that the functions
({\bf u}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{1\leq j\leq q}{1\leq j\leq q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq j\leq q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq j\leq q$}}}
are
orthonormal in
{\bf L}_{\mathchoice{2}{2}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}}[0,1]
, i.e.

S(t)=\sum^{q}_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}\beta_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}{\bf u}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(t)\,.

(5.6)

We use the estimator (
4.4
)
to estimate the parameters
\beta=(\beta_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{1\leq j\leq q}{1\leq j\leq q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq j\leq q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq j\leq q$}}}
as

\beta^{*}_{\mathchoice{\gamma}{\gamma}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\gamma$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\gamma$}}}=(\beta^{*}_{\mathchoice{\gamma,j}{\gamma,j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\gamma,j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\gamma,j$}}})_{\mathchoice{1\leq j\leq q}{1\leq j\leq q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq j\leq q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq j\leq q$}}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\beta^{*}_{\mathchoice{\gamma,j}{\gamma,j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\gamma,j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\gamma,j$}}}=({\bf u}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}},S^{*}_{\mathchoice{\gamma}{\gamma}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\gamma$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\gamma$}}})\,.

Moreover, we use the selection model procedure (
4.16
)
as

\beta^{*}=(\beta^{*}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{1\leq j\leq q}{1\leq j\leq q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq j\leq q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq j\leq q$}}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\beta^{*}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}=({\bf u}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}},S^{*})\,.

(5.7)

It is clear that

|\beta^{*}_{\mathchoice{\gamma}{\gamma}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\gamma$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\gamma$}}}-\beta|^{2}_{\mathchoice{q}{q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle q$}}}=\sum^{q}_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}(\beta^{*}_{\mathchoice{\gamma,j}{\gamma,j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\gamma,j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\gamma,j$}}}-\beta_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})^{2}=\|S^{*}_{\mathchoice{\gamma}{\gamma}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\gamma$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\gamma$}}}-S\|^{2}\quad\mbox{and}\quad|\beta^{*}-\beta|^{2}_{\mathchoice{q}{q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle q$}}}=\|S^{*}-S\|^{2}\,.

Therefore, Theorem
5.3
implies

Theorem 5.7
.

Assume that conditions
{\bf C}_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}})
-
{\bf C}_{\mathchoice{3}{3}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 3$}}})
,
{\bf D})
hold and the function (
5.6
) has the square integrable derivative
\dot{S}
. Then, for any
n\geq 3
,
p\geq 1

and the weight family
\Gamma
for which conditions
{\bf H}_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}})
-
{\bf H}_{\mathchoice{4}{4}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 4$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 4$}}})
hold also
and for any
0<\rho<1/2

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathchoice{Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}}{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q,\beta}{Q,\beta}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,\beta$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,\beta$}}}|\beta^{*}-\beta|^{2}_{\mathchoice{q}{q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle q$}}}

\displaystyle\leq\,\frac{1+5\rho}{1-\rho}\min_{\mathchoice{\gamma\in\Gamma}{\gamma\in\Gamma}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\gamma\in\Gamma$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\gamma\in\Gamma$}}}\,\sup_{\mathchoice{Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}}{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q,\beta}{Q,\beta}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,\beta$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,\beta$}}}|\beta^{*}-\beta|^{2}_{\mathchoice{q}{q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle q$}}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\rho n}\,{\bf U}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(1+\|\dot{S}\|^{2})\,,

(5.8)

where the term
{\bf U}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}
satisfies the property
(
5.2
).

Theorems

5.4
and
5.5

imply the efficiency property for the estimate (
5.7
) based on
the model selection procedure
(
4.16
)
constructed on the trigonometric basis functions (
3.3
) with the weight coefficients (
4.21
) and
the penalty threshold satisfying the
conditions (
5.5
).

Theorem 5.8
.

Assume that the conditions of Theorems

5.4
and
5.5
hold.
Then the estimate (
5.7
)
is asymptotically efficient, i.e.

\lim_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}\,v_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}^{2k/(2k+1)}\,\sup_{\mathchoice{S\in W_{\mathchoice{k,{\bf r}}{k,{\bf r}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}}}{S\in W_{\mathchoice{k,{\bf r}}{k,{\bf r}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle S\in W_{\mathchoice{k,{\bf r}}{k,{\bf r}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle S\in W_{\mathchoice{k,{\bf r}}{k,{\bf r}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}}$}}}\,\sup_{\mathchoice{Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}}{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q,\beta}{Q,\beta}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,\beta$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,\beta$}}}|\beta^{*}-\beta|^{2}_{\mathchoice{q}{q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle q$}}}=l_{\mathchoice{k}{k}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}({\bf r})

and

\lim_{\mathchoice{n\to\infty}{n\to\infty}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n\to\infty$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n\to\infty$}}}\frac{\inf_{\mathchoice{\widehat{\beta}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\in\Xi_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{\widehat{\beta}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\in\Xi_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widehat{\beta}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\in\Xi_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widehat{\beta}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}\in\Xi_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}}\,\sup_{\mathchoice{S\in W_{\mathchoice{k,{\bf r}}{k,{\bf r}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}}}{S\in W_{\mathchoice{k,{\bf r}}{k,{\bf r}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle S\in W_{\mathchoice{k,{\bf r}}{k,{\bf r}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle S\in W_{\mathchoice{k,{\bf r}}{k,{\bf r}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,{\bf r}$}}}$}}}\,\sup_{\mathchoice{Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q\in{\cal where
\Xi_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}
is the set of all possible estimators for the vector
\beta
.

Remark 5.4
.

In the estimator (
4.16
) doesn’t use the dimension
q
in (
5.6
). Moreover, it can be equal to
+\infty
.
In this case it is impossible to use neither LASSO method nor Danzig selector.

Remark 5.5
.

If in addition the functions
({\bf u}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{1\leq j\leq q}{1\leq j\leq q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1\leq j\leq q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq j\leq q$}}}
are orthonormal
on the grid
\{t_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}},\ldots t_{\mathchoice{p}{p}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle p$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle p$}}}\}
, i.e. possess the property (
3.1
), as, for example, in the trigonometric basis case
(
3.3
), we can use these functions
in (
3.5
).

6
Monte-Carlo simulations

In this section we give the results of numerical simulations to assess the performance and improvement of
the proposed model selection procedure (
4.16
).
We simulate the model (
1.3
) with

1
-periodic functions
S
of the forms

S_{1}(t)=t\,\sin(2\pi t)+t^{2}(1-t)\cos(4\pi t)

(6.1)

and

S_{2}(t)=\sum^{+\infty}_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}\frac{1}{1+j^{3}}\sin(2\pi jt)

(6.2)

on
[0,\,1]
and the Ornstein – Uhlenbeck – Lévy noise process
\xi_{\mathchoice{t}{t}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t$}}}
is defined as

\mathrm{d}\xi_{\mathchoice{t}{t}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t$}}}=-\xi_{\mathchoice{t}{t}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t$}}}\mathrm{d}t+0.5\,\mathrm{d}w_{\mathchoice{t}{t}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t$}}}+0.5\,\mathrm{d}z_{\mathchoice{t}{t}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t$}}}\,,\quad z_{\mathchoice{t}{t}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t$}}}=\sum^{N_{\mathchoice{t}{t}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t$}}}}_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}\,Y_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\,,

where
N_{\mathchoice{t}{t}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t$}}}
is a homogeneous Poisson process of intensity
\lambda=1
and

(Y_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{j\geq 1}{j\geq 1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j\geq 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j\geq 1$}}}
is i.i.d.

{\cal N}(0,\,1)
sequence (see, for example,
[
26
]
).

We use the model selection procedure (
4.16
) with the weights (
4.20
) in which

k^{*}=100+\sqrt{\ln(n+1)}
,
r_{i}=i/\ln(n+1)
,
m=[\ln^{2}(n+1)]
,
\varsigma^{*}=0.5
and
\rho=(3+\ln n)^{-2}
.
We define the empirical risk as

{\cal R}(S^{*},\,S)=\frac{1}{p}\sum_{\mathchoice{j=1}{j=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j=1$}}}^{p}\widehat{{\bf E}}\Delta^{2}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(t_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})\quad\mbox{and}\quad\widehat{{\bf E}}\Delta^{2}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(t)=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\mathchoice{l=1}{l=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l=1$}}}^{N}\Delta^{2}_{\mathchoice{n,l}{n,l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n,l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n,l$}}}(t)\,,

where

\Delta_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}(t)=S_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}^{*}(t)-S(t)

and

\Delta_{\mathchoice{n,l}{n,l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n,l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n,l$}}}(t)=S_{\mathchoice{n,l}{n,l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n,l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n,l$}}}^{*}(t)-S(t)
is the deviation for the
l
-th replication. In this example we take
p=10001
and

N=1000
.

Remark 6.1
.

Figures show the behavior of
the procedures (
4.1
) and (
4.16
) depending
on the values of observation periods
n
.
The bold lines are the functions (
6.1
) and (
6.2
),
the continuous lines are the model selection
procedures based on the
least squares estimates
\widehat{S}

and the dashed lines are the improved model selection procedures
S^{*}
.
From the Table 2 for the same
\gamma
with various observations
numbers
n
we can conclude that
theoretical result on the improvement effect (
4.6
) is confirmed
by the numerical simulations.
Moreover, for the proposed shrinkage procedure, from the Table 1 and Figures 1–3,
we can conclude that the benefit is considerable for non large
n
.

7
Properties of the model (
1.1
) -(
2.5
)

In this section
we study the noise process (
2.5
)
with the conditions
(
2.6
)-(
2.8
). More precisely,
we check conditions
{\bf C}_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}})
and
{\bf C}_{\mathchoice{2}{2}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}})
for the trigonometric basis
(
3.3
).
First we study condition
{\bf C}_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}})
.

Proposition 7.1
.

There exists a constant
{\bf l}>0
such that for any
n\geq 1

\sup_{\mathchoice{Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q\in{\cal Q}_{\mathchoice{n}{n}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}}}$}}}{\bf L}_{1}(Q)\leq\,{\bf l}\,\varsigma^{*}\,,

(7.1)

where the parameter
\varsigma^{*}
is defined in (
2.7
).

Proof.
First
we note that Proposition
A.1
implies

{\bf E}_{\mathchoice{Q,S}{Q,S}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q,S$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q,S$}}}\xi^{2}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}=\sigma_{\mathchoice{Q}{Q}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle Q$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Q$}}}\left(1+\tau_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}\right)\,,

(7.2)

where
\tau_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}=n^{-1}\,\int^{n}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}\,\psi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(t)\check{\varepsilon}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t

and

\check{\varepsilon}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(t)=a\int^{t}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}\,e^{a(t-s)}\,\psi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(s)\,(1+e^{2as})\mathrm{d}s
.
It is easy to check
that
|\tau_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}}|\leq 2
.
For
j\geq 2
we represent
\tau_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}
and
\check{\varepsilon}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(t)
as

\tau_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}=\tau_{\mathchoice{1,j}{1,j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,j$}}}+\tau_{\mathchoice{2,j}{2,j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2,j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2,j$}}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\check{\varepsilon}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(t)=\varepsilon_{\mathchoice{1,j}{1,j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,j$}}}(t)+\varepsilon_{\mathchoice{2,j}{2,j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2,j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2,j$}}}(t)\,,

where

\varepsilon_{\mathchoice{1,j}{1,j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,j$}}}(t)=a\int^{t}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}\,e^{a(t-s)}\,\psi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(s)\,\mathrm{d}s
,

\varepsilon_{\mathchoice{2,j}{2,j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2,j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2,j$}}}(t)=a\int^{t}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}\,e^{a(t+s)}\,\psi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(s)\,\mathrm{d}s

\tau_{\mathchoice{1,j}{1,j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,j$}}}=\frac{1}{n}\int^{n}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}\,\psi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(t)\varepsilon_{\mathchoice{1,j}{1,j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,j$}}}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\quad\mbox{and}\quad\tau_{\mathchoice{2,j}{2,j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2,j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2,j$}}}=\frac{1}{n}\int^{n}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}\,\psi_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(t)\varepsilon_{\mathchoice{2,j}{2,j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2,j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2,j$}}}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\,.

(7.3)

In view of
\psi_{j}(t)=\sum_{k=1}^{np}\mbox{Tr}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(t_{k}){\bf 1}_{\mathchoice{(t_{k-1},t_{k}]}{(t_{k-1},t_{k}]}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle(t_{k-1},t_{k}]$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle(t_{k-1},t_{k}]$}}}(t)
the values
\tau_{\mathchoice{1,j}{1,j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,j$}}}
can be represented as

\tau_{\mathchoice{1,j}{1,j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,j$}}}=\frac{a}{n}\,\sum^{np}_{\mathchoice{k=1}{k=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k=1$}}}\,\sum^{k}_{\mathchoice{l=1}{l=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l=1$}}}\,\mbox{Tr}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(t_{\mathchoice{k}{k}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}})\,\mbox{Tr}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(t_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}})\,\varkappa_{\mathchoice{k,l}{k,l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,l$}}}\,,

where

\varkappa_{\mathchoice{k,l}{k,l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,l$}}}=\int^{t_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}}}_{\mathchoice{t_{\mathchoice{l-1}{l-1}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l-1$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l-1$}}}}{t_{\mathchoice{l-1}{l-1}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l-1$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l-1$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t_{\mathchoice{l-1}{l-1}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l-1$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l-1$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t_{\mathchoice{l-1}{l-1}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l-1$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l-1$}}}$}}}\,\left(\int^{t_{\mathchoice{k}{k}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}}}_{\mathchoice{t_{\mathchoice{k-1}{k-1}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k-1$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k-1$}}}}{t_{\mathchoice{k-1}{k-1}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k-1$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k-1$}}}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle t_{\mathchoice{k-1}{k-1}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k-1$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k-1$}}}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle t_{\mathchoice{k-1}{k-1}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k-1$}}{\lower 0.37672pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k-1$}}}$}}}\,(e^{a(t-s)}\,{\bf 1}_{\mathchoice{\{s\leq t\}}{\{s\leq t\}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\{s\leq t\}$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\{s\leq t\}$}}})\mathrm{d}t\right)\,\mathrm{d}s\,.

Integration yields

\varkappa_{\mathchoice{k,k}{k,k}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,k$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,k$}}}=\varkappa^{*}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}(p)=(e^{a/p}-1-a/p)a^{-2}

and for
1\leq l<k
the coefficients

\varkappa_{\mathchoice{k,l}{k,l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k,l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k,l$}}}=\varkappa^{*}_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}}(p)\,e^{at_{\mathchoice{k-l}{k-l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k-l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k-l$}}}}

with

\varkappa^{*}_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}}(p)=a^{-2}\,(e^{-a/p}-1)\,(1-e^{a/p})
. Therefore,

\tau_{\mathchoice{1,j}{1,j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1,j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1,j$}}}=a\varkappa^{*}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}(p)p+a\,\varkappa^{*}_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}}(p)\,p^{2}\,A_{\mathchoice{j,p}{j,p}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,p$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,p$}}}\,,

(7.4)

where

A_{\mathchoice{j,p}{j,p}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,p$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,p$}}}=\frac{1}{np^{2}}\,\sum^{np-1}_{\mathchoice{l=1}{l=1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l=1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l=1$}}}\,\upsilon_{\mathchoice{j,l}{j,l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,l$}}}\,,\quad\upsilon_{\mathchoice{j,l}{j,l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j,l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j,l$}}}=\sum^{np}_{\mathchoice{k=l+1}{k=l+1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k=l+1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k=l+1$}}}\,\mbox{Tr}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(t_{\mathchoice{k}{k}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}})\,\mbox{Tr}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}}(t_{\mathchoice{l}{l}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle l$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l$}}})\,e^{at_{\mathchoice{k-l}{k-l}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle k-l$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k-l$}}}}\,.

It should be noted that

0\leq\varkappa^{*}_{\mathchoice{0}{0}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}(p)\leq\frac{1}{2p^{2}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad 0\leq\varkappa^{*}_{\mathchoice{1}{1}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}}(p)\leq\,\frac{e^{a_{\mathchoice{max}{max}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle max$}}{\lower 0.5382pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle max$}}}}}{p^{2}}\,.

(7.5)

From the definition of the functions
(\mbox{Tr}_{\mathchoice{j}{j}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle j$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle j$}}})_{\mathchoice{2\leq j\leq p}{2\leq j\leq p}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 2\leq j\leq p$}}{\lower 0.75346pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 2\leq j\leq p$}}}

it follows that
